<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We disprove the unrestricted four-dimensional disk-embedding conjecture. We construct immersed disks in a compact oriented smooth four-manifold with framed algebraic dual spheres satisfying the usual equivariant intersection and reduced self-intersection conditions, but with no pairwise disjoint locally flat replacements that preserve the boundary maps and induced normal framings. The obstruction holds even when the replacement disks' relative homotopy classes are not prescribed.

- **claim-main-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist a compact connected oriented smooth four-manifold $M$ with boundary, an integer $k\geq1$, generically immersed proper disks $$f_1,\ldots,f_k:(D^2,S^1)\longrightarrow(M,\partial M)$$ with disjoint embedded boundary circles, and generically immersed framed spheres $g_1,\ldots,g_k:S^2\longrightarrow M$, such that $$\lambda(f_i,g_j)=\delta_{ij},\qquad \lambda(g_i,g_j)=0\quad\text{for all }i,j,\qquad \widetilde\mu(g_i)=0\quad\text{for all }i,$$ but there are no pairwise disjoint locally flat embedded disks in $M$ with the same boundary maps and the boundary normal framings induced by the $f_i$.
  - Explanation: A fixed disk problem in a compact oriented smooth four-manifold has diagonal algebraic duals with vanishing sphere pairings and reduced self-intersections, yet admits no disjoint locally flat replacement with the same boundary maps and framings.
- **claim-assumption-universal-replacement** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ass-disk)): Let $M$ be a compact connected oriented smooth four-manifold with boundary. Suppose that a finite collection of proper generic immersions $$f_i\colon(D^2,S^1)\longrightarrow(M,\partial M),\qquad 1\leq i\leq k,$$ has pairwise disjoint embedded boundary circles, and admits generically immersed framed spheres $g_1,\ldots,g_k$ such that $$\lambda(f_i,g_j)=\delta_{ij},\qquad \lambda(g_i,g_j)=0,\qquad \widetilde\mu(g_i)=0 \quad\text{for all }i,j.$$ Here intersections are counted in $\mathbb Z[\pi_1M]$, with fixed whiskers, the diagonal equations are included, and $$\widetilde\mu(g_i)\in \mathbb Z[\pi_1M]\big/ \big\langle h-h^{-1},\,\mathbb Z\cdot1\big\rangle$$ uses the additive quotient. Then the $f_i$ can be replaced by pairwise disjoint locally flat embedded disks with the same boundary maps and the same induced normal framings on those boundary circles.
  - Explanation: The universal framed disk replacement statement that the paper contradicts; it is used only to obtain replacement disks for one fixed problem.
- **claim-geometric-reduction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-geometric-reduction)): For the integers $m,p$ and the finite list just specified, there is a compact connected oriented smooth four-manifold $X$, constructed without Assumption <a href="#ass:disk" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ass:disk">2</a>, with the following properties.* *It has a regular cover with deck group $F_m$, whose block graph is the Cayley tree of $F_m$. Each block is a four-dimensional one-handlebody on the $p$ extra letters. Its exposed boundary piece is $$K_v\cong \bigl(\#^p(S^1\times S^2)\bigr) \setminus\operatorname{int}\nu(L_{2m}),$$ where $L_{2m}$ is a $2m$-component unlink in a ball. The port meridians and the extra letters freely generate $\pi_1K_v$. At every join, the meridian on one side is the longitude on the other. There is a finite family of framed grope bodies with boundary in these pieces, equivariant in the cover, realizing the list <a href="#eq:three-slot-inputs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:three-slot-inputs">[eq:three-slot-inputs]</a>.* *If Assumption <a href="#ass:disk" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ass:disk">2</a> holds, there are equivariant cuts, with compact quotient, having connected vertex pieces $V_v$ and connected edge three-manifolds $Y_e$. Each $Y_e$ has as its entire boundary the prescribed join torus. Every corresponding three-slot grope word is trivial in $\pi_1V_v$.* *In the index-two parity quotient, these cuts give two connected vertex pieces $A,B$ and $2m$ connected oriented edge pieces $Y_1,\ldots,Y_{2m}$. Their ambient manifold $X_2$ is homotopy equivalent to the graph with two vertices and $2m$ edges, with $p$ additional circles at each vertex. For any characteristic-zero field $k$, difference of restrictions gives $$\label{eq:geometry-mv-degree-two} H^2(A;k)\oplus H^2(B;k) \xrightarrow{\;\cong\;} \bigoplus_{i=1}^{2m}H^2(Y_i;k).$$ In degree one it is surjective, and restricts to an isomorphism on the kernel of evaluation on the $p$ extra loops at each vertex.* *At each boundary torus choose the two original coordinate loops $\alpha_i,\beta_i$ so that $\alpha_i$ has nonzero image in $H_1(Y_i;k)$ and $\beta_i$ has zero image. Such a choice is possible: one of the original coordinate directions, not just an arbitrary slope, is killed. Call the endpoint where $\beta_i$ is a longitude the *plus endpoint*; there $\alpha_i$ is the port meridian. There are exactly $m$ edges with plus endpoint $A$. After numbering these as $1,\ldots,m$, the finite relation list includes, at $A$, all the required three-slot relations on any three distinct $\alpha_i$, and, at $B$, those on the corresponding $\beta_i$, with independent conjugations by $\mathcal C$.
  - Explanation: A plumbing manifold with a regular cover over a free group carries finitely many marked grope bodies; replacement disks would yield connected equivariant cuts, vertex and edge pieces, and the stated cohomology and slope data.
- **lemma-marked-body** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-body)): The two-stage grope body used in <a href="#eq:grope-word-properties" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:grope-word-properties">[eq:grope-word-properties]</a> has a three-dimensional handlebody thickening which, with its three marked tip annuli, is a boundary join of three longitudinal-annulus collars. Finitely many copies can be placed disjointly in the boundary of the initial one-handlebody, with prescribed parallel tip tracks to the cancelling-handle attaching regions and with the external conjugations <a href="#eq:three-slot-inputs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:three-slot-inputs">[eq:three-slot-inputs]</a>. Their surface framings can be matched to the handle-core framings.
  - Explanation: The two-stage grope body thickens to a handlebody that is a boundary join of annulus collars, can be placed disjointly with prescribed paths and conjugations, and has surface framings matched to handle cores.
- **lemma-exterior-duals** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-exterior-duals)): There are proper generically immersed disks $f_j$ in $E$, with disjoint boundary circles and with the bottom-surgery framings, and framed spheres $g_j$ in $E$, such that $$\lambda(f_i,g_j)=\delta_{ij},\qquad \lambda(g_i,g_j)=0,\qquad \widetilde\mu(g_j)=0 .$$ All equalities hold over $\mathbb Z[\pi_1E]$.
  - Explanation: In the exterior of the bottom surfaces there are immersed disks and framed spheres with diagonal algebraic duality and vanishing sphere pairings and reduced self-intersections over the group ring.
- **lemma-connected-tree-cuts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-connected-tree-cuts)): Suppose the initial grope boundaries bound the disjoint disks just constructed. Then the boundary map to the plumbing tree extends equivariantly to a map whose cuts can be chosen to have the connected vertex and edge pieces asserted in Proposition <a href="#prop:geometric-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:geometric-reduction">3</a>. Every lifted disk lies in its prescribed vertex piece.
  - Explanation: Given the disjoint disks, an equivariant map to the tree yields cuts with connected vertex and edge pieces, each disk lying in its vertex piece.
- **lemma-geometric-edge-cohomology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-geometric-edge-cohomology)): The cohomology and slope conclusions of Proposition <a href="#prop:geometric-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:geometric-reduction">3</a> hold. For each edge piece one also has $$\label{eq:edge-relative-cohomology} H^1(Y_i,\partial Y_i;k) \cong\ker\bigl(H^1(Y_i;k)\longrightarrow H^1(\partial Y_i;k)\bigr), \qquad b_2(Y_i)=b_1(Y_i)-1.$$
  - Explanation: Mayer–Vietoris and duality give the restriction isomorphisms and the one-surviving-slope structure on each edge piece.
- **theorem-tensor-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-tensor-obstruction)): Let $h=(h_1,\ldots,h_n)$ be a finite list of coordinate blocks over a characteristic-zero field $K$, of total dimension $N$. Let $I\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}$ have cardinality $m\geq5$, and let $$F_i\in K\langle h_i\rangle\quad(1\leq i\leq n), \qquad b_i\in K\langle h_i\rangle\quad(i\in I).$$ Put $$T=K[t_i:i\in I]/(t_i^2:i\in I),\qquad S_0(h)=\sum_{i=1}^nF_i(h_i),\qquad S_t(h)=S_0(h)+\sum_{i\in I}t_i b_i(h_i).$$ Suppose there are formal parametrizations $$H_L(x,t)\in (x,t)\,T[[x]]^N,\qquad H_R(y)\in (y)\,K[[y]]^N,$$ where $x$ has $a$ coordinates and $y$ has $N-a$ coordinates, with the following properties.* 1. *The central tangent maps $$\partial_xH_L(0,0):K^a\longrightarrow K^N,\qquad \partial_yH_R(0):K^{N-a}\longrightarrow K^N$$ are injective and have complementary images.* 2. *The identities $$S_t(H_L(x,t))=0,\qquad S_0(H_R(y))=0$$ hold in $T[[x]]$ and $K[[y]]$, respectively.* 3. *For every three distinct $i,j,k\in I$, $$\begin{aligned} t_i t_j t_k &\in\bigl((\partial_{h_\nu}S_t)(H_L(x,t)): 1\leq\nu\leq N\bigr)\subset T[[x]], \label{eq:tensor-source-membership}\\ (b_i b_j b_k)(H_R(y)) &\in\bigl((\partial_{h_\nu}S_0)(H_R(y)): 1\leq\nu\leq N\bigr)\subset K[[y]]. \label{eq:tensor-target-membership} \end{aligned}$$* *Then these data do not exist.
  - Explanation: Complementary formal graphs with separated potentials, exact vanishing and triple gradient-ideal memberships cannot exist when at least five edges are active.
- **lemma-tensor-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-reduction)): If the data in Theorem <a href="#thm:tensor-obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:tensor-obstruction">8</a> existed, data satisfying the same conditions would exist over a field of positive characteristic $p$, for arbitrarily large $p$. The edge functions would still be separated in their original blocks.
  - Explanation: Artin approximation and finite specialization transfer the putative data to large positive characteristic, preserving the separation of edge functions.
- **remark-tensor-coefficient-field** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rem-tensor-coefficient-field)): The lemma also applies when $K=\mathbb C((\sigma))$. Only finitely many elements of $K$ occur as coefficients of the finite presentations, and each is treated as a single coefficient. Specialization is performed on $A$, not coefficientwise on Laurent series or on the whole field $K$. In particular, no boundedness of the negative $\sigma$-orders of the coefficients of an Artin solution is required. The algebraicity of the fixed separated potentials is essential: the argument does not assert that ordinary Artin approximation can algebraize arbitrary unknown formal potentials while preserving separate variable restrictions.
  - Explanation: The reduction also works over the Laurent series field because specialization is done on a finitely generated subalgebra.
- **lemma-tensor-frobenius** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-frobenius)): Every formal change of internal coordinates over $T_p$ that is centered modulo $(t)$ preserves the ideal $(z_1^p,\ldots,z_N^p)$. Hence it induces an automorphism of the truncated algebra and of its relative differential forms.
  - Explanation: Centered coordinate changes preserve the Frobenius truncation ideal and induce automorphisms of truncated forms.
- **lemma-tensor-graph-form** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-graph-form)): The form $U_c$ is $\mathrm{d}$-closed and is annihilated by the graph ideal. Every internal one-form whose restriction to the graph is zero wedges to zero with $U_c$. For two central graphs with complementary tangents and graph forms $U_0,V$, $$\label{eq:tensor-nonzero-pairing} \int U_0\wedge V\ne0 .$$
  - Explanation: The graph form is closed and killed by the graph ideal, and transverse central graphs pair nontrivially.
- **lemma-tensor-membership-boundary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-membership-boundary)): For every three distinct active indices $i,j,k$, there are actual forms $B_{ijk}$ and $C_{ijk}$ with $$\label{eq:tensor-actual-boundaries} t_i t_j t_k U_t=D_t B_{ijk},\qquad b_i b_j b_k V=D C_{ijk}.$$ The first equality is over the full parameter algebra $T_p$.
  - Explanation: Triple gradient-ideal memberships become actual boundary identities for the twisted differentials on the graph forms.
- **lemma-tensor-kernel-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-kernel-support)): Let $v\in\bigotimes_{i=1}^nH_i$ be killed by $B_iB_jB_k$ for every three distinct active indices. Then $v$ belongs to the sum of tensor subspaces having all but at most two active factors in the corresponding $K_i$.
  - Explanation: A tensor killed by all triple products lies in the sum of subspaces with all but at most two active factors in the kernels.
- **lemma-tensor-kernel-projection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-kernel-projection)): Let $(H,\delta)$ be a finite-dimensional graded complex over a field, and let $K\subset H$ be any graded subspace. There is a chain projection $P:H\to H$, homotopic to the identity, whose image is a space of representatives for $H(H,\delta)$ and which satisfies $$P(K)\subset K\cap\ker\delta .$$
  - Explanation: A chain projection homotopic to the identity sends any graded subspace into its intersection with the cycles.
- **lemma-edge-cohomology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-cohomology)): There are natural isomorphisms $$H^1(Y,T)\cong H^1(C),\qquad H^2(C)\cong H^2(Y),$$ and $H^0(C)=H^3(C)=0$. Both middle groups have dimension $n=b_1(Y)-1=b_2(Y)$. Every closed one-form in $C$ has zero actual boundary pullback. Integration gives a perfect pairing $$H^1(C)\otimes H^2(C)\longrightarrow\mathbb C.$$
  - Explanation: The boundary complex computes relative and absolute cohomology in the middle degrees, with a perfect integration pairing.
- **lemma-edge-central-ideal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-central-ideal)): The curvature ideal of $s(h)$ is the Jacobian ideal $$J=\mathop{\mathrm{Jac}}(f)=\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial h_1},\ldots, \frac{\partial f}{\partial h_N}\right).$$ Moreover $f\in\mathfrak{m}^3$.
  - Explanation: The curvature ideal of the central slice equals the Jacobian ideal of the Chern–Simons potential, which has high order.
- **lemma-edge-affine-ideal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-affine-ideal)): The function $$\label{eq:edge-adjusted-potential} f_\tau(h)=\mathcal C(s(h,\tau)) +\tfrac{\epsilon}{2}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(u v(h,\tau))$$ has internal Jacobian ideal equal to the full curvature ideal of the affine slice. Here the internal derivatives are only the derivatives with respect to the $h_j$.
  - Explanation: The boundary-adjusted potential has internal Jacobian ideal equal to the full curvature ideal of the affine slice.
- **lemma-edge-based-slice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-based-slice)): The central slice identifies its curvature-zero scheme with the formal scheme of based representations of $\pi_1Y$ satisfying $G_\alpha=1$. Over the dual numbers, the affine slice identifies its curvature-zero scheme with the based representations satisfying $G_\alpha=\exp(-\tau T_0)$. These statements hold on all local Artinian coefficient algebras, not only on reduced points.
  - Explanation: Curvature-zero schemes of the slices are the formal schemes of based representations with the prescribed peripheral holonomy, nilpotents included.
- **prop-edge-potential** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-edge-potential)): With the boundary polarization fixed above, there is a formal potential $f\in\mathbb C[[h_1,\ldots,h_N]]$, where $N=3(b_1(Y)-1)$, and a slice $s(h)$ such that:* 1. *$f=\mathcal C(s)$ has order at least three. Its full Jacobian ideal equals the curvature ideal of $s$. The formal critical scheme $\mathop{\mathrm{Crit}}(f)=\mathop{\mathrm{Spf}}(S/\mathop{\mathrm{Jac}}(f))$ is the based flat-representation scheme with $G_\alpha=1$, and carries its marked based matrix $G_\beta$.* 2. *For each fixed $T_0\in\mathfrak{g}$ there is an affine slice with boundary $\tau T_0\zeta+v(h,\tau)\xi$ and adjusted potential $$f_\tau=f+\tau b =\mathcal C(s(h,\tau)) +\tfrac{\epsilon}{2}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\tau T_0v(h,\tau)), \qquad \tau^2=0.$$ Its internal Jacobian ideal equals its full curvature ideal. Accordingly $\mathop{\mathrm{Crit}}_h(f_\tau)$ is the based flat-representation scheme with $G_\alpha=\exp(-\tau T_0)$. No derivative with respect to $\tau$ is imposed in this relative critical scheme.* 3. *On the entire, possibly nonreduced, central critical scheme, $$\label{eq:edge-log-coefficient} b=\epsilon\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(T_0v(h,0)) =-\epsilon\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(T_0\log G_\beta\bigr) \quad\bmod\mathop{\mathrm{Jac}}(f).$$ The function $b$ has order at least two. Every flat connection with the indicated normalized boundary value is carried uniquely to the corresponding slice by gauge transformations equal to the identity on $T$.
  - Explanation: A marked formal edge potential exists whose critical scheme is the based flat-representation scheme, with an affine first-order variation giving the logarithmic holonomy coefficient.
- **theorem-edge-algebraicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-edge-algebraicity)): Put $N=\dim(H^1(C)\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_2)$, $R=\mathbb C[[h_1,\ldots,h_N]]$, and $J=\mathop{\mathrm{Jac}}(f)$. There are an invertible formal coordinate change $x=\varphi(h)$ and an algebraic power series $F(x)\in(x)^3$ such that $$f(h)=F(\varphi(h)).$$ Fix a torus basepoint and any finite collection of based loops in $Y$. For every matrix entry of their holonomies on the central critical scheme $\mathop{\mathrm{Spf}}(R/J)$, there is an algebraic power series in the $x$-coordinates whose pullback is that entry. In particular this holds for the based $\beta$-holonomy. The coordinate change can be chosen to identify these markings on the entire, possibly nonreduced, critical scheme.
  - Explanation: After a formal coordinate change the edge potential becomes algebraic and the marked holonomy entries on the full critical scheme are algebraic.
- **prop-formal-trace-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-formal-trace-comparison)): Let $X$ be a finite triangulated space, and let $$a_X\in \bigl(A_X\otimes\mathfrak g\widehat\otimes\mathfrak m_R\bigr)^1, \qquad (d_X+Q)a_X+\tfrac12[a_X,a_X]=0,$$ represent a family of local systems formally near the trivial one. For its evaluation map $\operatorname{ev}_{a_X}:X_B\times\mathop{\mathrm{Spf}}R\to BG$, the image of $\operatorname{ev}_{a_X}^*\omega_\kappa$ under the Betti coefficient map of (Pantev et al. 2013, sec. 2.1) [[PTVV2013](#ref-PTVV2013)] is the class of $$\label{eq:formal-trace-representative} \Theta_X=\tfrac12\kappa(\delta_Ra_X,\delta_Ra_X), \qquad (d_X+\mathcal L_Q)\Theta_X=0,\qquad \delta_R\Theta_X=0,$$ in the full complex $\operatorname{Cl}^2_R(A_X)$. This identification is natural for source restrictions, derived coefficient changes, and integration of source cochains.* *For an oriented compact three-manifold $Z$ with boundary $S$, it also identifies the relative-orientation isotropic homotopy with integration of $\Theta_Z$ over $Z$. If $S=S^2$ and the sphere condition is represented by $a_S=\nu b$, where $\nu$ is a two-form and $b$ has internal degree $-1$, then $\Theta_S=0$ strictly. Using the zero isotropic path on this sphere model, the resulting intersection form is represented by $$\tfrac12\int_Z \kappa(\delta_Ra_Z,\delta_Ra_Z)$$ as a closed two-form of degree $-1$.
  - Explanation: The shifted closed form pulled back by evaluation is represented by the integrated trace form, compatibly with relative integration.
- **lemma-cartan-jacobian-square** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cartan-jacobian-square)): Suppose that $Qh=0$, $Qq\in(h)^2$, and that strict closed $(-1)$-shifted two-forms $\omega,\omega'$ on $R[q]$ are homotopic as closed forms. If $\iota_Q\omega=\delta f$ and $\iota_Q\omega'=\delta f'$, then, after normalizing constants, $$f-f'\in(Qq_1,\ldots,Qq_N)^2.$$
  - Explanation: Hamiltonians of homotopic closed forms differ by an element of the square of the Jacobian ideal.
- **lemma-jacobian-square-flow** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-jacobian-square-flow)): Let $f\in(h)^3$ and $e\in\mathop{\mathrm{Jac}}(f)^2$. There is a formal automorphism $\Phi$ of $R$, equal to the identity modulo $J=\mathop{\mathrm{Jac}}(f)$, such that $$(f+e)\circ\Phi=f.$$
  - Explanation: An error in the square of the Jacobian ideal is removed by a formal automorphism that is the identity on the critical scheme.
- **prop-vertex-data** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-vertex-data)): Suppose that the data of Proposition <a href="#prop:geometric-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:geometric-reduction">3</a> exist, with $m\geq 5$, $p\geq9$ extra letters, and the finite family of independently conjugated boundary laws described there. Their Brunnian property means that omitting any slot makes the law the identity, as in Equation <a href="#eq:grope-word-properties" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:grope-word-properties">[eq:grope-word-properties]</a>. There are a characteristic-zero field $K$, internal coordinate blocks $h_i$ for the $2m$ edges, and algebraic power-series germs $F_i(h_i)$ and $b_i(h_i)$, the latter for the $m$ active edges, with the following properties. Put $$T=K[t_i:i\text{ active}]/(t_i^2:i\text{ active}),\qquad S_0=\sum_i F_i, \qquad S_t=S_0+\sum_{i\text{ active}}t_i b_i.$$ There are formal parametrizations $H_L(x,t)$ over $T$ and $H_R(y)$ over $K$ whose central tangent maps are injective and have complementary images in the full internal coordinate space. They are smooth graph germs over $T$ and $K$, respectively, and $$\label{eq:vertex-final-vanishing} S_t(H_L)=0,\qquad S_0(H_R)=0.$$ For every three distinct active indices $i,j,k$, $$\begin{aligned} t_i t_j t_k &\in\left((\partial_{h_a}S_t)(H_L):a=1,\ldots,N\right) \subset T[[x]], \label{eq:vertex-final-source}\\ (b_i b_j b_k)(H_R) &\in\left((\partial_{h_a}S_0)(H_R):a=1,\ldots,N\right) \subset K[[y]], \label{eq:vertex-final-target} \end{aligned}$$ where $N$ is the total number of internal coordinates. The field can be taken to be $K=\mathbb C((\sigma))$. The individual functions can be normalized so that $F_i(0)=b_i(0)=0$.
  - Explanation: The cut data produce separated algebraic edge functions and complementary graph parametrizations with exact vanishing and the triple memberships required by the tensor obstruction.
- **lemma-vertex-linear** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-vertex-linear)): After fixing the evaluations on the extra letters and on all plus meridians, the direct sum of the two vertex degree-one spaces maps isomorphically, by difference of restrictions, to $\mathcal H$. Moreover, $$\label{eq:vertex-h2-isomorphism} H^2(A;\mathbb C)\oplus H^2(B;\mathbb C) \longrightarrow\bigoplus_i H^2(Y_i;\mathbb C)$$ is an isomorphism. In particular each vertex summand maps injectively.
  - Explanation: Difference of restrictions is an isomorphism in degree one after fixing evaluations, and in degree two.
- **lemma-vertex-offshell-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-vertex-offshell-identities)): At the joint origin the tangent images of $H_A$ and $H_B$, with the external parameters fixed, are complementary. For each vertex, $$\label{eq:vertex-square-and-gradient} S_P(H_P)\in I_P^2, \qquad (\partial_h S_P)(H_P)=M_P r_P,$$ where $M_P$ is an actual coefficient matrix whose reduction at the joint origin has full column rank $q_P$. Consequently the restricted gradient ideal equals $I_P$.
  - Explanation: Vertex tangent images are complementary, the potential lies in the square of the residual ideal, and the restricted gradient ideal equals the residual ideal.
- **lemma-vertex-correction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-vertex-correction)): Let $R$ be a complete Noetherian local $\mathbb Q$-algebra with maximal ideal $\mathfrak m_R$, let $I=(r_1,\ldots,r_q)$, let $S\in R[[h_1,\ldots,h_N]]$, and let $H\in\mathfrak m_R^N$. Internal derivatives are taken over $R$, and substitution at $H$ is $\mathfrak m_R$-adically continuous. Suppose $S(H)\in I^2$ and $(\partial_h S)(H)=Mr$, with a unit full-column minor in $M$. Assume also that $I\subset\mathfrak m_R^2$ and the internal Hessian of $S$ evaluated at $H$ has entries in $\mathfrak m_R$. Then there is a convergent correction of $H$ by $I$-multiples making $S(H)=0$. It is unchanged modulo $I$ and to first order at the origin; the gradient still generates $I$. The functions $r_a$ on the parameter domain are held fixed throughout the correction.
  - Explanation: A Newton-type correction by multiples of the residual ideal achieves exact vanishing without changing the residual ideal or the first-order tangent.
- **lemma-vertex-adjoint-span** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-vertex-adjoint-span)): The nine matrices $\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(g_{ab}(\sigma))$ span $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_K(\mathfrak{g}\otimes K)$.
  - Explanation: The nine explicit adjoint matrices span all endomorphisms of the Lie algebra over the Laurent field.
- **lemma-vertex-triple-ideals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-vertex-triple-ideals)): For any three distinct active edges, the product of their three source parameters belongs to $I_A$. At $B$, every product of one matrix entry from each of their three logarithmic $\beta$ holonomies belongs to $I_B$. The same conclusion holds with entries of $G_i-1$ in place of the logarithmic holonomies.
  - Explanation: The Brunnian property forces triple products of source parameters and of holonomy entries into the residual ideals.
- **lemma-tensor-nilpotent-correction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-nilpotent-correction)): Let $I$ be a finite set of cardinality $m$, put $T=K[t_i:i\in I]/(t_i^2:i\in I)$, let $J=(t_i:i\in I)$, and work in $T[[x]]$. Fix a finite residual list $r$, with ideal $\mathcal I=(r)$. Suppose a formal section $H$ and a formal potential $S$ satisfy $$S(H)\in J\mathcal I^2,\qquad (\partial_h S)(H)=M r,$$ where $M$ has a unit full-column minor. Then a displacement of $H$ in $J\mathcal I$ makes $S(H)=0$ identically and keeps the restricted gradient ideal equal to $\mathcal I$. If the original central parametrization has an injective tangent map, the corrected section is still a smooth formal graph over $T$.
  - Explanation: Over the square-zero parameter algebra an error in the nilpotent ideal times the squared residual ideal is corrected while keeping gradient generation and the graph property.
- **corollary-vertex-tensor-interface** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-vertex-tensor-interface)): The cut data of Proposition <a href="#prop:geometric-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:geometric-reduction">3</a>, with $m\geq5$ and the indicated finite law list, supply all the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:tensor-obstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:tensor-obstruction">8</a>.
  - Explanation: The cut data supply every hypothesis of the finite tensor obstruction.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-geometric-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "For the integers $m,p$ and the finite list just specified, there is a compact connected oriented smooth four-manifold $X$, constructed without Assumption <a href=\"#ass:disk\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ass:disk\">2</a>, with the following properties.*\n\n*It has a regular cover with deck group $F_m$, whose block graph is the Cayley tree of $F_m$. Each block is a four-dimensional one-handlebody on the $p$ extra letters. Its exposed boundary piece is $$K_v\\cong\n \\bigl(\\#^p(S^1\\times S^2)\\bigr)\n \\setminus\\operatorname{int}\\nu(L_{2m}),$$ where $L_{2m}$ is a $2m$-component unlink in a ball. The port meridians and the extra letters freely generate $\\pi_1K_v$. At every join, the meridian on one side is the longitude on the other. There is a finite family of framed grope bodies with boundary in these pieces, equivariant in the cover, realizing the list <a href=\"#eq:three-slot-inputs\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:three-slot-inputs\">[eq:three-slot-inputs]</a>.*\n\n*If Assumption <a href=\"#ass:disk\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ass:disk\">2</a> holds, there are equivariant cuts, with compact quotient, having connected vertex pieces $V_v$ and connected edge three-manifolds $Y_e$. Each $Y_e$ has as its entire boundary the prescribed join torus. Every corresponding three-slot grope word is trivial in $\\pi_1V_v$.*\n\n\n*In the index-two parity quotient, these cuts give two connected vertex pieces $A,B$ and $2m$ connected oriented edge pieces $Y_1,\\ldots,Y_{2m}$. Their ambient manifold $X_2$ is homotopy equivalent to the graph with two vertices and $2m$ edges, with $p$ additional circles at each vertex. For any characteristic-zero field $k$, difference of restrictions gives $$\\label{eq:geometry-mv-degree-two}\n H^2(A;k)\\oplus H^2(B;k)\n       \\xrightarrow{\\;\\cong\\;}\n       \\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2m}H^2(Y_i;k).$$ In degree one it is surjective, and restricts to an isomorphism on the kernel of evaluation on the $p$ extra loops at each vertex.*\n\n*At each boundary torus choose the two original coordinate loops $\\alpha_i,\\beta_i$ so that $\\alpha_i$ has nonzero image in $H_1(Y_i;k)$ and $\\beta_i$ has zero image. Such a choice is possible: one of the original coordinate directions, not just an arbitrary slope, is killed. Call the endpoint where $\\beta_i$ is a longitude the *plus endpoint*; there $\\alpha_i$ is the port meridian. There are exactly $m$ edges with plus endpoint $A$. After numbering these as $1,\\ldots,m$, the finite relation list includes, at $A$, all the required three-slot relations on any three distinct $\\alpha_i$, and, at $B$, those on the corresponding $\\beta_i$, with independent conjugations by $\\mathcal C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "The two-stage grope body used in <a href=\"#eq:grope-word-properties\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:grope-word-properties\">[eq:grope-word-properties]</a> has a three-dimensional handlebody thickening which, with its three marked tip annuli, is a boundary join of three longitudinal-annulus collars.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-exterior-duals",
        "exact_quote": "There are proper generically immersed disks $f_j$ in $E$, with disjoint boundary circles and with the bottom-surgery framings, and framed spheres $g_j$ in $E$, such that $$\\lambda(f_i,g_j)=\\delta_{ij},\\qquad\n \\lambda(g_i,g_j)=0,\\qquad\n \\widetilde\\mu(g_j)=0 .$$ All equalities hold over $\\mathbb Z[\\pi_1E]$.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-tensor-obstruction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h=(h_1,\\ldots,h_n)$ be a finite list of coordinate blocks over a characteristic-zero field $K$, of total dimension $N$. Let $I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,n\\}$ have cardinality $m\\geq5$, and let $$F_i\\in K\\langle h_i\\rangle\\quad(1\\leq i\\leq n),\n \\qquad b_i\\in K\\langle h_i\\rangle\\quad(i\\in I).$$ Put $$T=K[t_i:i\\in I]/(t_i^2:i\\in I),\\qquad\n S_0(h)=\\sum_{i=1}^nF_i(h_i),\\qquad\n S_t(h)=S_0(h)+\\sum_{i\\in I}t_i b_i(h_i).$$ Suppose there are formal parametrizations $$H_L(x,t)\\in (x,t)\\,T[[x]]^N,\\qquad\n H_R(y)\\in (y)\\,K[[y]]^N,$$ where $x$ has $a$ coordinates and $y$ has $N-a$ coordinates, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The central tangent maps $$\\partial_xH_L(0,0):K^a\\longrightarrow K^N,\\qquad\n      \\partial_yH_R(0):K^{N-a}\\longrightarrow K^N$$ are injective and have complementary images.*\n\n2.  *The identities $$S_t(H_L(x,t))=0,\\qquad S_0(H_R(y))=0$$ hold in $T[[x]]$ and $K[[y]]$, respectively.*\n\n\n\n3.  *For every three distinct $i,j,k\\in I$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n     t_i t_j t_k\n     &\\in\\bigl((\\partial_{h_\\nu}S_t)(H_L(x,t)):\n                        1\\leq\\nu\\leq N\\bigr)\\subset T[[x]],\n           \\label{eq:tensor-source-membership}\\\\\n     (b_i b_j b_k)(H_R(y))\n     &\\in\\bigl((\\partial_{h_\\nu}S_0)(H_R(y)):\n                        1\\leq\\nu\\leq N\\bigr)\\subset K[[y]].\n           \\label{eq:tensor-target-membership}\n     \n    \\end{aligned}$$*\n\n*Then these data do not exist.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-tensor-obstruction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $h=(h_1,\\ldots,h_n)$ be a finite list of coordinate blocks over a characteristic-zero field $K$, of total dimension $N$. Let $I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,n\\}$ have cardinality $m\\geq5$, and let $$F_i\\in K\\langle h_i\\rangle\\quad(1\\leq i\\leq n),\n \\qquad b_i\\in K\\langle h_i\\rangle\\quad(i\\in I).$$ Put $$T=K[t_i:i\\in I]/(t_i^2:i\\in I),\\qquad\n S_0(h)=\\sum_{i=1}^nF_i(h_i),\\qquad\n S_t(h)=S_0(h)+\\sum_{i\\in I}t_i b_i(h_i).$$ Suppose there are formal parametrizations $$H_L(x,t)\\in (x,t)\\,T[[x]]^N,\\qquad\n H_R(y)\\in (y)\\,K[[y]]^N,$$ where $x$ has $a$ coordinates and $y$ has $N-a$ coordinates, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The central tangent maps $$\\partial_xH_L(0,0):K^a\\longrightarrow K^N,\\qquad\n      \\partial_yH_R(0):K^{N-a}\\longrightarrow K^N$$ are injective and have complementary images.*\n\n2.  *The identities $$S_t(H_L(x,t))=0,\\qquad S_0(H_R(y))=0$$ hold in $T[[x]]$ and $K[[y]]$, respectively.*\n\n\n\n3.  *For every three distinct $i,j,k\\in I$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n     t_i t_j t_k\n     &\\in\\bigl((\\partial_{h_\\nu}S_t)(H_L(x,t)):\n                        1\\leq\\nu\\leq N\\bigr)\\subset T[[x]],\n           \\label{eq:tensor-source-membership}\\\\\n     (b_i b_j b_k)(H_R(y))\n     &\\in\\bigl((\\partial_{h_\\nu}S_0)(H_R(y)):\n                        1\\leq\\nu\\leq N\\bigr)\\subset K[[y]].\n           \\label{eq:tensor-target-membership}\n     \n    \\end{aligned}$$*\n\n*Then these data do not exist.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $h=(h_1,\\ldots,h_n)$ be a finite list of coordinate blocks over a characteristic-zero field $K$, of total dimension $N$. Let $I\\subset\\{1,\\ldots,n\\}$ have cardinality $m\\geq5$, and let $$F_i\\in K\\langle h_i\\rangle\\quad(1\\leq i\\leq n),\n \\qquad b_i\\in K\\langle h_i\\rangle\\quad(i\\in I).$$ Put $$T=K[t_i:i\\in I]/(t_i^2:i\\in I),\\qquad\n S_0(h)=\\sum_{i=1}^nF_i(h_i),\\qquad\n S_t(h)=S_0(h)+\\sum_{i\\in I}t_i b_i(h_i).$$ Suppose there are formal parametrizations $$H_L(x,t)\\in (x,t)\\,T[[x]]^N,\\qquad\n H_R(y)\\in (y)\\,K[[y]]^N,$$ where $x$ has $a$ coordinates and $y$ has $N-a$ coordinates, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The central tangent maps $$\\partial_xH_L(0,0):K^a\\longrightarrow K^N,\\qquad\n      \\partial_yH_R(0):K^{N-a}\\longrightarrow K^N$$ are injective and have complementary images.*\n\n2.  *The identities $$S_t(H_L(x,t))=0,\\qquad S_0(H_R(y))=0$$ hold in $T[[x]]$ and $K[[y]]$, respectively.*\n\n\n\n3.  *For every three distinct $i,j,k\\in I$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n     t_i t_j t_k\n     &\\in\\bigl((\\partial_{h_\\nu}S_t)(H_L(x,t)):\n                        1\\leq\\nu\\leq N\\bigr)\\subset T[[x]],\n           \\label{eq:tensor-source-membership}\\\\\n     (b_i b_j b_k)(H_R(y))\n     &\\in\\bigl((\\partial_{h_\\nu}S_0)(H_R(y)):\n                        1\\leq\\nu\\leq N\\bigr)\\subset K[[y]].\n           \\label{eq:tensor-target-membership}\n     \n    \\end{aligned}$$*\n\n*Then these data do not exist.",
      "explanation": "Complementary formal graphs with separated potentials, exact vanishing and triple gradient-ideal memberships cannot exist when at least five edges are active.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

        "anchor": "stmt-rem-tensor-coefficient-field",
        "exact_quote": "The lemma also applies when $K=\\mathbb C((\\sigma))$. Only finitely many elements of $K$ occur as coefficients of the finite presentations, and each is treated as a single coefficient. Specialization is performed on $A$, not coefficientwise on Laurent series or on the whole field $K$. In particular, no boundedness of the negative $\\sigma$-orders of the coefficients of an Artin solution is required. The algebraicity of the fixed separated potentials is essential: the argument does not assert that ordinary Artin approximation can algebraize arbitrary unknown formal potentials while preserving separate variable restrictions.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-edge-potential",
        "exact_quote": "With the boundary polarization fixed above, there is a formal potential $f\\in\\mathbb C[[h_1,\\ldots,h_N]]$, where $N=3(b_1(Y)-1)$, and a slice $s(h)$ such that:*\n\n1.  *$f=\\mathcal C(s)$ has order at least three. Its full Jacobian ideal equals the curvature ideal of $s$. The formal critical scheme $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Crit}}(f)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spf}}(S/\\mathop{\\mathrm{Jac}}(f))$ is the based flat-representation scheme with $G_\\alpha=1$, and carries its marked based matrix $G_\\beta$.*\n\n2.  *For each fixed $T_0\\in\\mathfrak{g}$ there is an affine slice with boundary $\\tau T_0\\zeta+v(h,\\tau)\\xi$ and adjusted potential $$f_\\tau=f+\\tau b\n        =\\mathcal C(s(h,\\tau))\n                 +\\tfrac{\\epsilon}{2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(\\tau T_0v(h,\\tau)),\n      \\qquad \\tau^2=0.$$ Its internal Jacobian ideal equals its full curvature ideal. Accordingly $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Crit}}_h(f_\\tau)$ is the based flat-representation scheme with $G_\\alpha=\\exp(-\\tau T_0)$. No derivative with respect to $\\tau$ is imposed in this relative critical scheme.*\n\n\n3.  *On the entire, possibly nonreduced, central critical scheme, $$\\label{eq:edge-log-coefficient}\n      b=\\epsilon\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}(T_0v(h,0))\n          =-\\epsilon\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}\\bigl(T_0\\log G_\\beta\\bigr)\n           \\quad\\bmod\\mathop{\\mathrm{Jac}}(f).$$ The function $b$ has order at least two. Every flat connection with the indicated normalized boundary value is carried uniquely to the corresponding slice by gauge transformations equal to the identity on $T$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-edge-algebraicity",
        "exact_quote": "Put $N=\\dim(H^1(C)\\otimes\\mathfrak{sl}_2)$, $R=\\mathbb C[[h_1,\\ldots,h_N]]$, and $J=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Jac}}(f)$. There are an invertible formal coordinate change $x=\\varphi(h)$ and an algebraic power series $F(x)\\in(x)^3$ such that $$f(h)=F(\\varphi(h)).$$ Fix a torus basepoint and any finite collection of based loops in $Y$. For every matrix entry of their holonomies on the central critical scheme $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spf}}(R/J)$, there is an algebraic power series in the $x$-coordinates whose pullback is that entry. In particular this holds for the based $\\beta$-holonomy. The coordinate change can be chosen to identify these markings on the entire, possibly nonreduced, critical scheme.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-formal-trace-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a finite triangulated space, and let $$a_X\\in\n  \\bigl(A_X\\otimes\\mathfrak g\\widehat\\otimes\\mathfrak m_R\\bigr)^1,\n \\qquad\n (d_X+Q)a_X+\\tfrac12[a_X,a_X]=0,$$ represent a family of local systems formally near the trivial one. For its evaluation map $\\operatorname{ev}_{a_X}:X_B\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spf}}R\\to BG$, the image of $\\operatorname{ev}_{a_X}^*\\omega_\\kappa$ under the Betti coefficient map of (Pantev et al. 2013, sec. 2.1) [[PTVV2013](#ref-PTVV2013)] is the class of $$\\label{eq:formal-trace-representative}\n \\Theta_X=\\tfrac12\\kappa(\\delta_Ra_X,\\delta_Ra_X),\n \\qquad\n (d_X+\\mathcal L_Q)\\Theta_X=0,\\qquad \\delta_R\\Theta_X=0,$$ in the full complex $\\operatorname{Cl}^2_R(A_X)$. This identification is natural for source restrictions, derived coefficient changes, and integration of source cochains.*\n\n*For an oriented compact three-manifold $Z$ with boundary $S$, it also identifies the relative-orientation isotropic homotopy with integration of $\\Theta_Z$ over $Z$. If $S=S^2$ and the sphere condition is represented by $a_S=\\nu b$, where $\\nu$ is a two-form and $b$ has internal degree $-1$, then $\\Theta_S=0$ strictly. Using the zero isotropic path on this sphere model, the resulting intersection form is represented by $$\\tfrac12\\int_Z\n                     \\kappa(\\delta_Ra_Z,\\delta_Ra_Z)$$ as a closed two-form of degree $-1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-formal-trace-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a finite triangulated space, and let $$a_X\\in\n  \\bigl(A_X\\otimes\\mathfrak g\\widehat\\otimes\\mathfrak m_R\\bigr)^1,\n \\qquad\n (d_X+Q)a_X+\\tfrac12[a_X,a_X]=0,$$ represent a family of local systems formally near the trivial one. For its evaluation map $\\operatorname{ev}_{a_X}:X_B\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spf}}R\\to BG$, the image of $\\operatorname{ev}_{a_X}^*\\omega_\\kappa$ under the Betti coefficient map of (Pantev et al. 2013, sec. 2.1) [[PTVV2013](#ref-PTVV2013)] is the class of $$\\label{eq:formal-trace-representative}\n \\Theta_X=\\tfrac12\\kappa(\\delta_Ra_X,\\delta_Ra_X),\n \\qquad\n (d_X+\\mathcal L_Q)\\Theta_X=0,\\qquad \\delta_R\\Theta_X=0,$$ in the full complex $\\operatorname{Cl}^2_R(A_X)$. This identification is natural for source restrictions, derived coefficient changes, and integration of source cochains.*\n\n*For an oriented compact three-manifold $Z$ with boundary $S$, it also identifies the relative-orientation isotropic homotopy with integration of $\\Theta_Z$ over $Z$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-cartan-jacobian-square",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $Qh=0$, $Qq\\in(h)^2$, and that strict closed $(-1)$-shifted two-forms $\\omega,\\omega'$ on $R[q]$ are homotopic as closed forms. If $\\iota_Q\\omega=\\delta f$ and $\\iota_Q\\omega'=\\delta f'$, then, after normalizing constants, $$f-f'\\in(Qq_1,\\ldots,Qq_N)^2.$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-jacobian-square-flow",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f\\in(h)^3$ and $e\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Jac}}(f)^2$. There is a formal automorphism $\\Phi$ of $R$, equal to the identity modulo $J=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Jac}}(f)$, such that $$(f+e)\\circ\\Phi=f.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-305.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-305.2"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-vertex-data",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that the data of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:geometric-reduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:geometric-reduction\">3</a> exist, with $m\\geq 5$, $p\\geq9$ extra letters, and the finite family of independently conjugated boundary laws described there. Their Brunnian property means that omitting any slot makes the law the identity, as in Equation <a href=\"#eq:grope-word-properties\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:grope-word-properties\">[eq:grope-word-properties]</a>. There are a characteristic-zero field $K$, internal coordinate blocks $h_i$ for the $2m$ edges, and algebraic power-series germs $F_i(h_i)$ and $b_i(h_i)$, the latter for the $m$ active edges, with the following properties. Put $$T=K[t_i:i\\text{ active}]/(t_i^2:i\\text{ active}),\\qquad\n S_0=\\sum_i F_i,\n \\qquad S_t=S_0+\\sum_{i\\text{ active}}t_i b_i.$$ There are formal parametrizations $H_L(x,t)$ over $T$ and $H_R(y)$ over $K$ whose central tangent maps are injective and have complementary images in the full internal coordinate space. They are smooth graph germs over $T$ and $K$, respectively, and $$\\label{eq:vertex-final-vanishing}\n S_t(H_L)=0,\\qquad S_0(H_R)=0.$$ For every three distinct active indices $i,j,k$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n t_i t_j t_k\n &\\in\\left((\\partial_{h_a}S_t)(H_L):a=1,\\ldots,N\\right)\n       \\subset T[[x]],                                      \\label{eq:vertex-final-source}\\\\\n (b_i b_j b_k)(H_R)\n &\\in\\left((\\partial_{h_a}S_0)(H_R):a=1,\\ldots,N\\right)\n       \\subset K[[y]],                                      \\label{eq:vertex-final-target}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $N$ is the total number of internal coordinates. The field can be taken to be $K=\\mathbb C((\\sigma))$. The individual functions can be normalized so that $F_i(0)=b_i(0)=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-vertex-data",

They are smooth graph germs over $T$ and $K$, respectively, and $$\\label{eq:vertex-final-vanishing}\n S_t(H_L)=0,\\qquad S_0(H_R)=0.$$ For every three distinct active indices $i,j,k$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n t_i t_j t_k\n &\\in\\left((\\partial_{h_a}S_t)(H_L):a=1,\\ldots,N\\right)\n       \\subset T[[x]],                                      \\label{eq:vertex-final-source}\\\\\n (b_i b_j b_k)(H_R)\n &\\in\\left((\\partial_{h_a}S_0)(H_R):a=1,\\ldots,N\\right)\n       \\subset K[[y]],                                      \\label{eq:vertex-final-target}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $N$ is the total number of internal coordinates. The field can be taken to be $K=\\mathbb C((\\sigma))$. The individual functions can be normalized so that $F_i(0)=b_i(0)=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.2"

      "exact_statement": "Suppose that the data of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:geometric-reduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:geometric-reduction\">3</a> exist, with $m\\geq 5$, $p\\geq9$ extra letters, and the finite family of independently conjugated boundary laws described there.         "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a complete Noetherian local $\\mathbb Q$-algebra with maximal ideal $\\mathfrak m_R$, let $I=(r_1,\\ldots,r_q)$, let $S\\in R[[h_1,\\ldots,h_N]]$, and let $H\\in\\mathfrak m_R^N$. Internal derivatives are taken over $R$, and substitution at $H$ is $\\mathfrak m_R$-adically continuous. Suppose $S(H)\\in I^2$ and $(\\partial_h S)(H)=Mr$, with a unit full-column minor in $M$. Assume also that $I\\subset\\mathfrak m_R^2$ and the internal Hessian of $S$ evaluated at $H$ has entries in $\\mathfrak m_R$. Then there is a convergent correction of $H$ by $I$-multiples making $S(H)=0$. It is unchanged modulo $I$ and to first order at the origin; the gradient still generates $I$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **unrestricted disk-embedding conjecture** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The *unrestricted disk-embedding conjecture* considered here is the extension of that theorem obtained by removing only the good-group hypothesis.
- **capped grope** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A capped grope here is a surface tree with disks attached at its terminal curves; its boundary records an iterated commutator in the terminal curves.
- **three-slot grope word** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A word $R$ in three free generators $X_1,X_2,X_3$ will be called a *three-slot grope word* if it is the boundary word of the rooted surface tree consisting of a punctured torus with a single tip on its first branch and a second punctured torus on its other branch.
- **ports** ([source](#eq:three-slot-inputs)): Each plumbing block will have $2m$ solid-torus boundary regions, called *ports*, through which it joins other blocks.
- **potential** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *potential* is a function in finitely many formal coordinates whose gradient equations describe these flat connections.
- **active edge** ([source](#sec:vertices)): An edge is *active* when its plus endpoint is at $A$.

| q-adjoint-determinant | determinant of the nine vectorized adjoint matrices | = | $512\sigma^{18}$ | nine chosen conjugators |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-marked-bodies** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-body) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-body): Thicken the two-stage grope body, place the finite family with based paths, and match cap framings by twisting. -> disjoint marked bodies with matched framings
- **step-exterior-duals** [claim](#stmt-lem-exterior-duals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exterior-duals): Compress the rim torus along the upper contraction and cancel paired sheet contributions in the group ring. -> disks and framed spheres with diagonal duality
- **step-tree-cuts** [claim](#stmt-lem-connected-tree-cuts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-connected-tree-cuts): Extend the boundary map equivariantly to the tree, take regular-value cuts and merge closed hanging pieces. -> connected vertex and edge pieces
- **step-edge-cohomology** [claim](#stmt-lem-geometric-edge-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-geometric-edge-cohomology): Mayer–Vietoris on the parity quotient plus Poincaré–Lefschetz duality on edge pieces. -> restriction isomorphisms and slopes
- **step-edge-ideal** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-central-ideal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-central-ideal): Contraction recursion identifies curvature coefficients with gradient entries of the Chern–Simons functional. -> Jacobian equals curvature ideal
- **step-edge-potential** [claim](#stmt-prop-edge-potential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-edge-potential): Adjust the potential by the boundary term and read the first-order holonomy coefficient. -> marked edge potential
- **step-edge-algebraicity** [claim](#stmt-thm-edge-algebraicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-jacobian-square-flow-2): Darboux chart, trace comparison, Cartan square-Jacobian estimate and flow correction. -> algebraic potential with marked holonomies
- **step-vertex-data** [claim](#stmt-prop-vertex-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tensor-nilpotent-correction-2): Build vertex slices, correct to exact vanishing, and use Brunnian laws for triple memberships. -> graphs satisfying tensor hypotheses
- **step-tensor-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-thm-tensor-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tensor-kernel-projection-2): Reduce to positive characteristic, truncated Koszul forms, kernel projection and a three-parameter lift forcing a vanishing pairing. -> nonexistence of the data
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-vertex-tensor-interface-2): Assume replacements exist, derive the vertex data, and contradict the tensor obstruction. -> the counterexample

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-obstruction-needs-five-active-edges**: The finite tensor obstruction excludes the graph parametrizations, exact vanishing identities and triple gradient-ideal memberships only when the active index set has at least five elements. Fewer active edges are not covered by the argument.
- **inv-plumbing-parameters**: The plumbing construction fixes the number of ports and extra letters before any cut is chosen. The marked grope bodies do not depend on the cuts.
- **inv-adjoint-span**: The nine explicit adjoint operators span the full endomorphism algebra of the Lie algebra over the Laurent field. This is what lets the independent conjugations detect every triple coefficient.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-good-groups**: Applying the counterexample to a manifold whose fundamental group is known to be good, for example of subexponential growth. -> The result removes only the good-group hypothesis from the disk-embedding statement; it says nothing about settings where the good-group theorem applies, so such a use would contradict established theorems.
- **anti-surgery-sequences**: Citing the theorem as a counterexample to the ordinary or simple surgery sequence, or to five-dimensional topological s-cobordism. -> The paper disproves only the geometric disk replacement problem; transferring that to surgery or cobordism needs an extra implication that is not proved.
- **anti-assumption-as-theorem**: Treating the universal disk replacement assumption as a proved statement and deriving consequences from it. -> The assumption is the statement refuted by the paper; consequences derived from it are vacuous or contradictory.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is a nonexistence statement proved by contradiction: it assumes disjoint locally flat replacement disks exist and derives an algebraic impossibility through formal power series, shifted symplectic charts and approximation theorems. No finite procedure computes an output from an input, so no runnable reference implementation faithfully represents it.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a non-existence theorem for embedded disk replacements. It gives no finite procedure that a program could run, so no software component follows from it. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper treats topological existence of locally flat disks in four-manifolds. Nothing in it concerns simulation, physics or game mechanics. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No explicit procedure is given that a verification or topology tool could implement. The construction is a one-off proof device that is applied to a hypothetical set of replacement disks and ends in a contradiction. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument has an explicit finite algebraic step, but it is used only to derive a contradiction from hypothetical data. It is not a procedure that computes an output, and it is not a decision procedure for disk replacement. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure is introduced. The plumbing tree and the cut pieces are proof objects, not structures for storage or lookup. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No computation, parallel scheme or hardware-relevant quantity appears. The finite tensor space is a proof device only. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Positive characteristic and finite fields appear only as an intermediate step of the obstruction. No hardness assumption, protocol or primitive is involved. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, data set or model. The topic is purely geometric and algebraic. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Potentials and critical schemes appear, but only as formal power series whose gradient ideals describe flat connections. There is no objective to optimise and no feasibility problem to solve. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-active-edge-threshold** (hypothesis): The finite tensor obstruction is proved only when the active index set has at least five elements. The argument gives no information for fewer active edges. Blocked use: ['Applying the obstruction to configurations with four or fewer active edges', 'Reading the theorem as a statement about every number of edges']
- **limit-char-zero-separated-blocks** (hypothesis): The obstruction is stated for a finite list of coordinate blocks over a characteristic-zero field, with each edge function depending only on its own block. Mixing the blocks inside the potentials is outside the statement. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem over a field of positive characteristic as the ground field', 'Applying it to potentials that couple several edge blocks']
- **limit-nonconstructive-contradiction** (nonconstructive): The main counterexample is obtained by assuming that disjoint locally flat replacement disks exist and deriving a contradiction. No replacement disks, cuts or algebraic data are ever produced explicitly. Blocked use: ['Extracting an algorithm or a computable certificate from the proof', 'Treating the cut pieces and graph parametrizations as objects that can be computed']
- **limit-assumption-only-refuted** (formal_scope): The universal framed disk replacement assumption is introduced only so that it can be contradicted. Statements derived from it hold only inside that proof by contradiction. Blocked use: ['Citing the assumption as a proved theorem', 'Deriving further results from it outside the contradiction argument']
- **limit-geometric-scope-only** (counterexample_scope): The conclusion concerns geometric disk replacement for the constructed disk problem. The paper explicitly makes no counterexample claim for surgery sequences or five-dimensional topological s-cobordism, which would need an additional implication. Blocked use: ['Citing the result as a counterexample to the ordinary or simple surgery sequence', 'Citing the result as a counterexample to five-dimensional topological s-cobordism']
- **limit-good-group-hypothesis-removed** (hypothesis): The refuted conjecture is the one obtained by removing only the good-group hypothesis. The result is a limit on dropping that hypothesis and gives no information where it holds. Blocked use: ['Applying the counterexample to manifolds whose fundamental group is good', 'Concluding failure of the established disk-embedding theorems for good groups']
- **limit-locally-flat-output** (edge_case): The hypothetical output disks are only locally flat, while the ambient construction and the cuts are smooth. Smooth forms and Stokes arguments are used on the pieces, never on the disks. Blocked use: ['Assuming the replacement disks are smooth', 'Applying smooth-form calculations directly to the replacement disks']
- **limit-fixed-algebraic-potentials** (approximation): Artin approximation is applied only to the final graph equations, with fixed and separately algebraic edge functions. The paper does not claim that arbitrary formal potentials can be algebraized while keeping separate variable restrictions. Blocked use: ['Applying Artin approximation to the earlier arbitrary formal potentials', 'Applying it coefficientwise to Laurent series']
- **limit-correction-hessian-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The Newton-type correction needs the residual ideal inside the square of the maximal ideal and the internal Hessian with entries in the maximal ideal. Without them the convergence argument is not available. Blocked use: ['Correcting a section whose residual list has order below two', 'Using the lemma when the Hessian entries are not in the maximal ideal']
- **limit-kernel-not-subcomplex** (edge_case): The kernels of the multiplication operators are not asserted to be subcomplexes for the induced differential. The projection lemma is needed precisely because of this. Blocked use: ['Assuming kernel factors are closed under the induced differential', 'Skipping the compatible projection step in the tensor argument']
- **limit-no-decision-procedure** (nonconstructive): The tensor theorem states that the hypothesised data do not exist. It is not a procedure that decides whether a given disk problem is solvable. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem as a solvability test for arbitrary disk problems', 'Implementing it as a general-purpose obstruction detector']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We disprove the unrestricted four-dimensional disk-embedding conjecture. We construct immersed disks in a compact oriented smooth four-manifold with framed algebraic dual spheres satisfying the usual equivariant intersection and reduced self-intersection conditions, but with no pairwise disjoint locally flat replacements that preserve the boundary maps and induced normal framings. The obstruction holds even when the replacement disks' relative homotopy classes are not prescribed.
